# [CHAPTER 10](#toc.xhtml#c10)

# [Legal and Environmental Aspects](#toc.xhtml#c10)

# [Introduction](#toc.xhtml#s342a)

Cryptocurrency is a new way of payment that has emerged in recent times, as an advancement in Virtual Currency. The use and functioning of Cryptocurrency are fairly different than traditional mode of payment and is not regulated by any government or financial institution.

There are no rules and regulations or any guidelines laid down for regulating and settling disputes while dealing with cryptocurrency. India recently decided to tax digital assets such as cryptocurrencies and non-fungible tokens (NFT), with a 30-percent tax on transfer of such assets as well as a 1-percent tax deduction at source (TDS) on every transaction. We shall read about NFT in this chapter.

# [Structure](#toc.xhtml#s343a)

In this chapter, we will cover the following topics:

*   Legal Aspects of Cryptocurrencies
*   The Transfer of Property
*   Digital Ownership and Non-Fungible Tokens (NFT)
*   Difference Between NFT and Cryptocurrency
*   Working and Use Cases for NFTs
*   Use Cases for NFTs
*   Electricity Consumption and Environmental Footprints in Usage in Blockchain Operations
*   Illicit Contents Scammers
*   Transfer of Funds
*   Preventing Money Laundering by Blockchain Technology
*   Exchange of Blockchain
*   Metaverse

# [Legal Aspects of Cryptocurrencies](#toc.xhtml#s344a)

All **Fiat** (the conventional) currencies in the world derive their authority as medium of transaction from the government or monetary authorities. For example, each Rupee (paper currency of India) is guaranteed to be returned by Reserve bank of India, the same way as, dollar bill by the Federal Reserve of USA. The currency we use is Fiat currency (money), a government-issued currency, is not backed by a commodity such as gold in all countries. Because central banks can regulate the amount of money generated, fiat money provides them more control over the economy and, consequently leads to inflation.

# [Legal Status of Cryptocurrencies in Major Countries of the World](#toc.xhtml#s345a)

Cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin and Ethereum are not backed or financially supported by any public or private entities. Throughout the world, it is almost difficult to make a case for their legal status in different financial jurisdictions. It is a universal fact that cryptocurrencies are largely functioning outside existing financial infrastructures. The legal status of cryptocurrencies is controversial for their use in daily transactions and trading.

All users as well as financial and banking organizations have come to the conclusion that cryptocurrencies cannot be classified as money, assets, or commodities since they lack intrinsic value and underlying cash flows. They are also similar to Ponzi schemes, if not worse.

In this aspect, the regulators fear the misuse of cryptocurrency as it may erode their attempts to combat money laundering by taking advantage of its anonymity. India, being a part of the Financial Action Task Force (FATF), has to abide by its standards that seek global cooperation between sovereign member nations.

As we know, cryptocurrencies are decentralized systems where transactions are authenticated by participants through consensus. They are cleverly designed to bypass the financial system and all its controls and cannot be traced, confiscated, or frozen by governments. However, in the budget of India (2022), it was made clear that the RBI would launch India’s Digital Rupee in FY 2023.

In June 2019, the Financial Action Task Force (FATF) of USA recommended that the wire transfers of cryptocurrencies should be subject to the requirements of its Travel Rule, which requires AML compliance. A **wire transfer** is an electronic way to transfer money and is also known as bank transfer. This mode of transfer is fast and secure and happens from one bank to another.

In the USA, the Bank Secrecy Act, among other things, requires financial institutions, including broker-dealers, to develop and implement anti-money laundering (AML) compliance programs.

El Salvador was the only country in the world to allow Bitcoin as a legal tender for monetary transactions. Japan’s Payment Services Act defines Bitcoin as legal property.

However as of April 2022, Cryptocurrency exchanges operating in the country are subject to collect information about the customer and details relating to the wire transfer. **China** has banned cryptocurrency exchanges and mining within its borders.

Notably, cryptocurrencies are legal in the European Union. Derivatives and other products that use cryptocurrencies will need to be certified as “financial instruments.” In June 2021, the European Commission released the Markets in Crypto-Assets (MiCA) regulation that lays out guidelines and regulatory safeguards for companies or vendors providing financial services using cryptocurrencies.

The United States of America is known as the biggest and most sophisticated financial market in the world. Crypto derivatives such as Bitcoin futures are available on the Chicago Mercantile Exchange. But the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) has iterated that Bitcoin and Ethereum are not securities.

Although cryptocurrencies are considered a form of money, the **Internal Revenue Service** (IRS) treats them as a financial *asset or property*. Additionally, just as with most investments, the government wants a cut of your profits if you sell or trade cryptocurrencies and make capital gains. On May 20, 2021, the U.S. Department of the Treasury announced a proposal that would require taxpayers to report any cryptocurrency transaction of and above $10,000 to the IRS. How the IRS would treat the tax proceeds—as capital gains or ordinary income—depends on how long the taxpayer held the cryptocurrency.

# [The Transfer of Property](#toc.xhtml#s346a)

Property is defined by any item that a person or a business has legal title over. Example of Property include tangible items, such as houses, cars, or appliances, or it can also refer to intangible items that carry the promise of future worth, such as stock and bond certificates. Property is mainly divided into two parts, namely movable and immovable. Any offence which is committed in regard to any property whether it is movable or immovable is punishable under the provisions of the law of Crimes or the Indian Penal Code \[IPC\].

The term transfer of property includes transfer through sale, mortgage, lease, actionable claim, gift, or exchange. The Act does not cover transfers by the operation of law, in the form of inheritance, forfeiture, insolvency, or sale through the execution of a decree.

The Transfer of Property Act 1882 is an Indian legislation which regulates the transfer of property in India. It contains specific provisions regarding what constitutes a transfer and the conditions attached to it. It came into force on 1 July 1882.

According to this Act, ‘transfer of property’ means an action by which a person conveys the property to one or more persons, or herself/himself. The act of transfer may be done in the present or for the future. The term person may include an individual, company or association, body of individuals, and any kind of property may be transferred, including the transfer of immovable property.

Property is broadly classified into the following categories:

*   Immovable Property (excluding standing timber, growing crops, and grass)
*   Movable Property (such as watch, timber)

If we interpret the Act, it means “immovable property does not include standing timber, growing crops or grass”. Section 3(26), The General Clauses Act, 1897, defines, “ immovable property” shall include land, benefits to arise out of the land, and things attached to the earth, or permanently fastened to anything attached to the earth. Also, The Registration Act, 1908, 2(6) states that “immovable property” includes land, buildings, hereditary allowances, rights to ways, lights, ferries, fisheries or any other benefit to arise out of the land, and things attached to the earth or permanently fastened to anything which is attached to the earth, but not standing timber, growing crops nor grass.

Note that a transfer of property passes forthwith to the transferee all the interest which the transferor is then capable of passing in the property unless a different intention is expressed or implied.

As per Section 43 of the Transfer of Property Act 1882:

*   In case a person either fraudulently or erroneously represents that she/he is authorized to transfer certain immovable property and does some acts to transfer such property for consideration, then such a transfer will continue to operate in future. It will operate on any interest which the transferor may acquire in such property.
*   This will be at the option of the transferee and can be done during the time in which the contract of transfer exists. As per this rule, the rights of the bona fide transferee, who has no notice of the earlier transfer or of the option, are protected. This rule embodies a rule of estoppel that means a person who makes a representation cannot later on go against it.
*   Every person who is competent to contract, is competent to transfer property, which can be transferred in whole or in part. He should be entitled to the transferable property, or authorized to dispose of transferable property which is not his own. The right may be either absolute or conditional, and the property may be movable or immovable, present or future. Such a transfer can be made orally unless a transfer in writing is specifically required under any law.
*   Even when a person is mentally competent, but physically unable to sign any contract, the property lawyer hired by him, can do that with the help of a power of attorney.

According to Section 6 of the Transfer of Property Act, property of any kind may be transferred. However, the person insisting non-transferability, must prove the existence of some law or custom which restricts the right of transfer. Unless there is some legal restriction preventing the transfer, the owner of the property can transfer it. However, in some cases, there may be a transfer of property by an unauthorized person who subsequently acquires an interest in such property.

In case the property transferred is subject to the condition which absolutely restrains the transferee from parting with or disposing of his interest in the property, the condition is void. The only exception is in the case of a lease where the condition is for the benefit of the lessor or those claiming under him. Generally, only the person having interest in the property is authorized to transfer and can pass on the proper title to any other person.

The rights of the transferees will not be adversely affected, provided: they acted in good faith; the property was acquired for a consideration, and the transferees had acted without notice of the defect in the title of the transferor.

These conditions must be satisfied:

*   It is compulsory that there must be a representation by the transferor that he has authority to transfer the immovable property.
*   The representation should be either fraudulent or erroneous.
*   The transferee must act on the representation in good faith.
*   The transfer should be done for a consideration.
*   The transferor should subsequently acquire some interest in the property he had agreed to transfer. The transferee may have the option to acquire the interest which the transferor subsequently acquires.

# [Digital Ownership and Non-Fungible Tokens (NFT)](#toc.xhtml#s347a)

The digital ownership gives authority to have permission to access and/or use data, information and knowledge about ourselves or about the objects someone own. Alternatively, it also grants or revokes rights on these data. The digital ownership is also a component layer of Internet of Things (IoT) that enables gathering of gigabytes of data on a single thing.

A Non-Fungible Tokens (NFT) is defined as a type of digital asset that represents real-world objects such as art, music, in-game items and videos. They are represented, bought and sold online, frequently with cryptocurrency, and they are generally encoded with the same underlying software as many cryptos have used. Needless to say, that NFTs are unique cryptographic tokens that exist on a blockchain and cannot be replicated.

Despite their existence since 2014, NFTs are currently gaining notoriety due to the fact that, despite their growing popularity, the majority of the capital invested in them comes from unapproved sources.

According to Forbes Advisor India (8.4.2022), a staggering $174 million has been spent on NFTs since November 2017.

Perhaps the most well-known NFT of 2021, “EVERYDAYS: The First 5000 Days,” was created by well-known digital artist Mike Winklemann, best known by his stage name “Beeple,” using a composite of 5,000 daily drawings. It sold at Christie’s for an astounding $610.3 million.

Anyone can view the individual images—or even the entire collage of images online for free. So why are people willing to spend millions on something they could easily screenshot or download?

The reason is that NFT allows the buyer to own the original item that contains built-in authentication, which serves as proof of ownership.

# [Difference Between NFT and Cryptocurrency](#toc.xhtml#s348a)

Physical currency (money) and cryptocurrencies are “fungible,” meaning they can be traded or exchanged for one another. Another word for fungible are exchangeable, substitutable, Inter-changeable or convertible. Crypto’s fungibility makes it a trusted means of conducting transactions on the blockchain.

NFT is built using the same kind of programming as cryptocurrency, such as Bitcoin or Ethereum, but that is where the similarity ends.

Each transaction of NFTs is different as they have a digital signature that makes it impossible for NFTs to be exchanged for or equal to one another (hence, non-fungible).

# [Working Method of NFT](#toc.xhtml#s349a)

NFTs exist on a blockchain, which is a distributed public ledger that records transactions. You are probably familiar with blockchain as the underlying process that makes cryptocurrencies possible[^(\[1\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn1). NFTs are typically held on the Ethereum blockchain, although other blockchains support them as well.

Typically, an NFT is created, or “minted”, from digital objects that represent both tangible and intangible items, including:

*   A unique digital artwork or Graphic art
*   Videos and sports highlights
*   Collectibles related with VIPS, Neta, Abhineta
*   Virtual avatars and video game skins
*   Designer sneakers
*   Music
*   A unique sneaker in a limited-run fashion line
*   An in-game item
*   An essay
*   A digital collectible
*   A domain name
*   A ticket that gives you access to an event or a coupon
*   Furniture
*   Memes
*   Big Sports Moments
*   Virtual Fashion
*   Real-world assets

Tweets count can also be included. Twitter co-founder Jack Dorsey sold his first ever tweet as an NFT for more than $2.9 million.

# [Use Cases for NFTs](#toc.xhtml#s350a)

The following factors are required to be kept in mind while thinking of owning NFTs:

*   NFTs are like physical collector’s items, only digital. So instead of getting an actual oil painting to hang on the wall, the buyer gets a digital file instead.
*   They also get exclusive ownership rights. NFTs can have only one owner at a time, and their use of blockchain technology makes it easier to verify ownership and transfer tokens between owners.
*   The creator can also store specific information in an NFT’s metadata. For instance, artists can sign their artwork by including their signature in the file.
*   Blockchain technology and NFTs provide artists and content creators a unique opportunity to monetize their wares. For example, artists no longer have to rely on galleries or auction houses to sell their art. Instead, the artist can sell it directly to the consumer as an NFT, which also lets them keep more of the profits. In addition, artists can program in royalties so they will receive a percentage of sales whenever their art is sold to a new owner. This is an attractive feature as artists generally do not receive future proceeds after their art is first sold.
*   Even celebrities such as Snoop Dogg and Lindsay Lohan are jumping on the NFT bandwagon, releasing unique memories, artwork, and moments as securitized NFTs.

Nyan Cat, a 2011-era GIF of a cat with a pop-tart body, was sold for approximately $600,000 in February. And NBA Top Shot generated more than $500 million in sales as of late March. A single LeBron James highlight NFT fetched more than $200,000.

Users are now able to redeem the virtual currency through third-party vendors, peer-to-peer transfers, and full cash redemptions as the issuer’s virtual currency or token becomes more popular. Many exchanges offer fiat-to-cryptocurrency exchanges, trading, and investment services. This has changed digital currencies from being a means of prepaying for goods or services, that can be redeemed with a single company, to being a widely accepted proxy for real currency and a means of transmitting legal tender between multiple parties.

Issuers were required to follow state and federal money transmitter, and money service business laws in what are considered landmark cases for the SEC. Many cryptocurrency startups responded by simply shifting their operations to tax havens and off-shore zones, bypassing numerous financial services laws in the US, Canada, Europe, and Australia.

# [Digital Ownership with Blockchain](#toc.xhtml#s351a)

Cryptocurrency and tokens do not have any legal tender status in any jurisdiction. The Internal Revenue Service (IRS) of United States describes tokens and cryptocurrencies as digital representations of value that serve as means of exchange, accounts units, and stores of value.

Assets remain very simple and antiquated, based on current legal concepts about goods, value, and exchange. Thus, depending on the jurisdiction, cryptocurrency may or may not be considered money. The hybrid nature of digital ownership of virtual tokens and property makes this inconsistency even worse. Various countries’ laws depend on whether they consider cryptocurrency or token a commodity or a currency.

Cryptocurrencies and virtual goods defy categorization from a legal perspective because their legal status is open to many interpretations. This will likely continue to happen until the industry grows and lawmakers and other authorities are better equipped to predict and understand its details and consequences.

Indeed, the absence of regulations has not stopped anybody, including investors, from taking cryptocurrency in return for digital tokens, virtual products, or in-game items. Game producers are incorporating exclusive digital products into their game’s arena to entice users and establish closed economies. To purchase so-called digital assets, such as collectible (non-fungible) tokens like virtual cats, players fork over millions of dollars in real estate.

The blockchain game ‘’CryptoKitties’’ was created by Dapper Labs, a Canadian firm, using Ethereum. Players could generate, purchase, and sell non-fungible tokens in the game. These NFTs stand for digital felines. The Digital Felines is a marketing agency based in Thessaloniki, Greece, with the sole purpose of creating, managing, and expanding your business’s image.

Soon after CryptoKitties, other blockchain and game developers began to offer eager fans spells, weapons, textures, skins, property, islands, and other digital assets.

Despite the lack of governance, investors and the general public continue to accept cryptocurrencies for the exchange and purchase of virtual products, in-game items, and digital tokens. A growing number of developers are adding exclusive digital goods to their games in an effort to pique players’ interest and establish “closed economies.” Millions are spent by players on so-called “digital assets,” including virtual kittens and other collectible, non-fungible tokens.

NFT proponents assert that the technique is groundbreaking in two respects. First of all, it assists artists, particularly up-and-coming ones, in escaping the grasp of intermediaries such as record labels and art galleries, who have traditionally kept the majority of the earnings for themselves. Direct interaction and mutually beneficial agreements might be made between the artists and the audience. A wide range of celebrities, including Ellen DeGeneres, Eminem, and Salman Khan, have joined the NFT marketplace.

CryptoKitties is among the initial endeavors to utilize blockchain technology for leisure and entertainment purposes. The Ethereum network experienced congestion in December 2017 due to the game’s popularity, which resulted in an all-time high in transactions and markedly slowed down the network[^(\[2\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn2). Virtual cats with unique visual characteristics and differing degrees of rarity can be bought, bred, and traded by players. To play the game, players must buy the cryptocurrency Ether, which they must use to complete all trading and breeding tasks.

The underlying blockchain network of Ethereum powers CryptoKitties’ operations. Every CryptoKitty token is non-fungible (NFT). Everyone is distinct, belongs to the user, is verified by the blockchain, and is subject to market fluctuations in value. Even the game developers are unable to copy or redistribute CryptoKitties without the user’s consent.

**Is Ownership of Digital Items Real or Fake?**

The practice of using real money to buy virtual products, in-game items, tokens, and digital currencies is becoming more and more common, despite the lack of regulations.

Whether it is $11,000 for an Eve Online Revenant Titan, $170,000 for a virtual cat on CryptoKitties, or $38,000 for a Dota 2 courier, people are spending a lot of money on digital goods, with the intention of reselling them.

In theory, each of these objects is valuable—at least to the individuals who paid for them. However, governments are still at odds over whether to classify that value as a commodity or as money. This distinction is crucial because, if they were seen as currency, they would be governed by a central bank, but if they were regarded as commodities, they would be subject to regulation by the appropriate commodity authority and would have tax ramifications.

Apps, media, in-game goods, and cryptocurrency libraries are being gathered by users, who may then give, share, and sell these collections to other users. However, the terms and conditions that users accept when they register for a game or service almost always specify that the digital goods, they purchase are non-transferable. The question here is whether the real accounts are transferable or not. Other kinds of accounts have highly defined but intangible values, such digital exchange profiles that hold a certain quantity of cryptocurrencies.

The European Court’s 2012 Oracle v. UsedSoft decision made it lawful to sell used software, including digital and physical games, as long as the seller removed it from their computer. Rentals of used software were nonetheless prohibited.

However, downloaded digital content was not covered by a June 2014 verdict from the regional court in Hamm, Germany. Furthermore, the law can be confusing when it comes to digital goods and in-game purchases, even though it is generally straightforward about music, movies, and books.

The increasing popularity and global pervasiveness of virtual assets will inevitably lead to a heightened awareness of digital ownership concerns. Governments will eventually need to have a crucial conversation about what they should and should not be able to control, as well as the best ways to go about it.

A boutique law company in San Francisco, The Gamma Law represents a restricted group of clients in innovative industries. They push the boundaries of innovation, give their clients the support they need to flourish in complicated and dynamic business situations, and help them accomplish their goals both domestically and globally.

# [Electricity Consumption and Environmental Footprints in Usage in Blockchain Operations](#toc.xhtml#s352a)

It is undeniable that Bitcoin has gone mainstream, within the past one decade, but perhaps not in quite the way Nakamoto imagined. According to Nakamoto’s founding white paper, the Bitcoin was to enable instantaneous, borderless transactions without the high fees or foreign exchange barriers that exist today.

When the enigmatic, possibly nonexistent Satoshi Nakamoto minted the world’s first cryptocurrency in 2009, the plan was to create a decentralized payments platform that would revolutionize the way we buy and sell everything[^(\[3\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn3).

Conceptually, it does not seem like Bitcoin should require enormous amounts of electricity. To purchase and sell bitcoin on your smartphone, all you have to do is point and click or tap. And for decades, we have had electronic networks that perform essentially the same functions for different kinds of digital transactions.

This method did not require nation-state levels of electricity in the early days of Bitcoin. However, the arithmetic puzzles in the cryptocurrency are designed to get much, much tougher as more people fight to solve them. This dynamic will only get stronger as more people try to invest in Bitcoin.

Bitcoin demands computers to do ever-more difficult mathematical operations. Verifying transactions on centralized networks requires a far lower energy consumption than this proof of work consensus method.

New sets of transactions (blocks) are added to Bitcoin’s blockchain roughly after every 10 minutes by so-called miners. While working on the blockchain, these miners are not required to trust each other. The only thing miners have to trust is the code that runs Bitcoin. The code includes several rules to validate new transactions. For example, a transaction can only be valid if the sender actually owns the sent amount. Every miner individually confirms whether transactions adhere to these rules, eliminating the need to trust other miners.

Ever since its inception, Bitcoin’s trust-minimizing consensus has been enabled by its proof-of-work algorithm (vide *[Chapter 4, Distributed Consensus in Open Environments](#c04.xhtml)*). The machines performing this work are consuming huge amounts of energy while doing so. Moreover, the energy used is primarily sourced from fossil fuels. The Bitcoin Energy Consumption Index was created to provide insight into these amounts, and raise awareness on the unsustainability of the proof-of-work algorithm.

An increasing number of rigs, or dedicated computers, are joining the battle to combat this, putting enormous amounts of electrical and processing energy into the ecosystem in an attempt to be the first to solve Bitcoin math puzzles and get the associated prize. Although there may be hundreds of thousands of computers racing to solve the same problem, only one can ultimately receive the Bitcoin honorarium.

It also takes a very long time to complete this process: A Bitcoin transaction can take up to ten minutes. Other digital transactions, like those powered by Visa, take less than a second and use roughly 1/500,000 the energy because they rely on a centralized authority to verify transactions.

Instead of enabling regular transactions, cryptocurrencies, like Bitcoin, have mostly evolved into speculative assets (a kind of digital gold), drawing investors who think they may sell their holdings for significant gains in the future. However, because the Bitcoin gold rush involves tremendous electricity usage, it has had a severe ecological impact on humanity.

The Fortune magazine states that each Bitcoin transaction consumes 1,173 kilowatt hours of electricity. That is the volume of energy that could “power the typical American home for six weeks,” the authors add. The Bitcoin mining that enables a purchase, sale or transfer, it posits, uses a punch of electricity that costs $176.

Based on the data from the Bitcoin Energy Consumption Index from Digiconomist, an online tool created by data scientist Alex de Vries, the carbon footprint of Bitcoin, the world’s largest cryptocurrency, is equivalent to that of New Zealand, with both emitting nearly 37 megatons of carbon dioxide into the atmosphere every year.

The Bitcoin Energy Consumption Index provides the latest estimate of the total energy consumption of the Bitcoin network.

The CBECI is an ongoing project created and maintained by the Digital Assets Programme (DAP) Team at the Cambridge Centre for Alternative Finance, an independent research institute based at The University of Cambridge, Judge Business School.

The Cambridge Bitcoin Electricity Consumption Index provides a real-time estimate of the total annual electricity consumption of the bitcoin network. The underlying model updates the network power estimate every thirty seconds based on live price and hash rate data.

Since 2020, Cambridge provides detailed insights into the localization of Bitcoin miners over time.

From Cambridge calculations, it is estimated that at an average efficiency of 48.7 it takes around 142,498 kWh to produce one bitcoin. This is equivalent to 142 MWh or 0.142 GWh. To get the daily amount of energy the network uses, we take the kWh to mine one bitcoin times the daily production rate of 900 coins.

It is essential to distinguish between electricity consumption and environmental footprint. The former concerns the total amount of electricity used by the Bitcoin mining process whereas, the latter is concerned with the environmental implications of Bitcoin mining. An environmental footprint takes into account the entirety of supply and demand of goods and services that is counted as energy used by any user or group of users. In doing so, it is assumed that the entire population follows a certain lifestyle characterized by a known person or a group of people. What ultimately matters for the environment is not the level of electricity consumption per se, but the carbon intensity of the energy sources used to generate that electricity.

Just to have an idea of this concept, let us use the annualized power consumption estimate from CBECI as of July 13th, 2021, which corresponds to roughly 70 TWh. Let us also assume that all this energy comes exclusively from coal (the most-polluting fossil fuel) and is generated in one of the world’s least efficient coal-fired power plants (the now-decommissioned Hazelwood Power Station in Victoria, Australia). In this worst-case scenario, the Bitcoin network would be responsible for about 111 million metric tons (Mt) of carbon dioxide emissions, accounting for roughly 0.35% of the world’s total yearly emissions.

Although Bitcoin is not unique among cryptocurrencies in terms of its environmental burden, but its high profile and uniquely inefficient transaction approval system makes it an easily condemnable scapegoat.

# [Combating Bitcoin’s Energy Problem](#toc.xhtml#s353a)

The energy consumption issue with Bitcoin, which is almost the size of a continent, may be resolved without forcing humanity to go back to centralized systems, as demonstrated by Visa’s network. As was the central promise of Bitcoin, the elimination of intermediaries like the card networks and their concentrated power over finance is needed. But if that is not possible then Bitcoin’s advocates have more than a few options:

*   Switching to Renewable Energy
*   Transiting to Proof-of-Stake Systems
*   Force Embracing Pre-Mining
*   Introduction Carbon Credits or Fees
*   Switch to Renewable Energy

In the year 2022, an estimated 39-percent of proof-of-work mining was performed using renewable energy. So, perhaps the most obvious and suggestable course of action was to enforce the user companies to adopt green means at the time of giving electric service connections to them by governments.

Countless startups have emerged to address this gap, each targeting new ways to bring more environmental friendly energy to Bitcoin. Hong Kong-based LiquidStack, aims to lower the temperature of mining rigs more efficiently, or Genesis Mining in Iceland, which exclusively uses renewable energy sources.

*   **Transition to Proof-of-Stake Systems**

According to Simon Peters, an eToro cryptocurrency market analyst, cryptocurrencies may transition from proof-of-work to proof-of-stake (see Review section 4.5 of this book), which does not necessitate the same frenzy of problem-solving.

The basic notion behind this is that in order to be eligible to validate transactions through proof-of-stake, network users must front a modest sum of cryptocurrency and enter a lottery.

The thought is that if you are putting up some amount of value as collateral, you are less likely to approve fraudulent transactions that would devalue the currency and cost you your stake.

In contrast to proof-of-work (PoW) systems, where a number of machines race to answer the same problem (spending energy), proof-of-stake systems eliminate the competitive computational element while saving energy and enabling each machine to work on one problem at a time. Whereas Proof of Work (POW) uses a competitive validation process to confirm transactions and add new blocks to the blockchain, Proof of Stake (POS) uses randomly chosen miners to validate transactions.

Ethereum, the blockchain system powering Ether and most NFTs, is in the process of converting to proof of stake from proof of work. This will dramatically reduce the energy consumption of Ethereum-based cryptos and blockchains by an estimated 910.5%.

*   **Embrace Pre-Mining**

To avoid the rush in wasteful computing involved in solving math problems quickly to earn digital coins, some cryptos have introduced pre-mining that is a system which functionally works similar to fiat currency or stocks. A central authority, such as the U.S. government (in the case of dollars) or a company (in the case of stocks), creates a set amount of an item and then carefully releases it into the economy depending on what is going on in the world or their business.

Several other crypto assets such as XRP (also popularly referred to as **Ripple**) were not mined at all but were instead produced algorithmically. This idea eliminates the need for dedicated high-speed mining equipment.

In these systems, transactions are still verified by a decentralized network of validators before they are added to the currency’s blockchain record. But those involved in the transaction may have to pay a small transaction fee to compensate the validators for their effort since the currency system itself does not always reward them. In the case of XRP, currently this fee is a fraction of a cent.

It is said that transitioning Bitcoin to a proof-of-stake or pre-mined system would not be easy. For making any fundamental change in Bitcoin protocol someone would have to convince the majority of miners to agree to the new system. This is rather a tough task when billions are at stake in the existing system that works slowly and electrically inefficiently. The last time a change of this magnitude happened, it was not accepted universally by miners, resulting in a so-called “hard fork” that created a separate cryptocurrency called Bitcoin Cash, which itself later hard forked into Bitcoin SV, among others.

*   **Introduce Carbon Credits or Fees**

The government-sanctioned ability of a company to emit a certain amount of carbon emission into the environment is called the Carbon credits. They remain often securitized, as such they can be traded (sold) to by companies that can afford to produce a lessor amount of emissions to other companies. Thus, this provides an incentive to a company to produce less than its allotment—as well as penalizes those that go over. In the case of a crypto mining company, this might mean it purchases carbon credits from another company to help offset the amount of emissions it creates or switches to greener energy so it can earn a profit from selling its credits.

# [The Environmental Future of the Blockchain](#toc.xhtml#s354a)

According to Digiconomis’s estimate, in year 2022, electricity costs consume up to an estimated 28-percent of Bitcoin mining’s profitability each year.

The smart contracts similar to those enabled by Ethereum will allow companies to automate much of their complex payment and business process systems by automatically checking to make sure that a purchase order, for example, complies with the terms and conditions of a contract. This might allow a company to reduce the number of employees who need to commute into an office to process orders, resulting in fewer transportation-related carbon emissions.

Though we may not know the full potential of green applications of blockchain technology for years to come, there are talks of using it to combat big issues, such as helping companies to better log carbon emissions or even, in a truly meta move, using blockchain-powered carbon credits to move to a carbon-neutral future.

Blockchain is a powerful tool that can significantly improve the transparency, accountability, and traceability of greenhouse gas emissions. It helps companies provide more accurate, reliable, standardized, and readily available data on carbon emissions. Blockchain has the potential to ease the process of finding and buying real estate through tokenization, using digital real estate tokens to represent physical assets. This would aid in easing transfers between the buyer and seller by cutting out the need for a middleman.

# [Illicit Contents](#toc.xhtml#s355a)

Illicit contents usually refer to something that is not morally proper or acceptable, and is considered improper or socially forbidden. Illicit activities such as smuggling or counterfeiting does happen but they are under the radar of the law. They may or may not be illegal but they go against social norms and values.

In *[Chapter 1, Overview of Blockchain Technology](#c01.xhtml)*, we learned that every Bitcoin transaction is stored in the distributed database known as the Bitcoin blockchain. However, people have found ways to hack the Bitcoin protocol to store more than just transactions.

Few years back, people started noticing all sorts of things embedded in the Bitcoin blockchain such as the Bitcoin logo, advertisements, and several prayers. There was even illegal pornography and leaked classified documents. All of these were put in by anonymous Bitcoin users. But none of this, so far, appears to seriously threaten those in power in governments and corporations. Once someone adds something to the Bitcoin ledger, it becomes sanctified that is sacrosanct.

Meanwhile, a 2014 tech blog (presented at the SXSW festival in the US) reveals the extraordinary range of material that the writer of the blog had already found stored in the Bitcoin blockchain[^(\[4\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn4).

This included photographs, illegal primes, an image of Nelson Mandela, Python software, leaked firmware keys, Valentine’s Day messages, Wikileaks data, an email, unknown encrypted content, and a Hex-encoded image of Ben Bernanke, then chairman of the Federal Reserve, and the original paper describing Bitcoin by its alleged founder, the pseudonymous Satoshi Nakamoto.

![](images/Figure-10.1.jpg)

**Figure 10.1:** Hacked copy of original paper describing Bitcoin by its alleged founder, the pseudonymous Satoshi Nakamoto

The initial cryptocurrency Bitcoin has raised financial concerns for governments worldwide. Despite its use for buying goods and services, there are still no uniform international laws that regulate Bitcoin or other cryptocurrencies. Many developed countries allow Bitcoin to be used, such as the U.S., Canada, and the U.K.

A fork in the blockchain is necessary to remove anything, causing Bitcoin to split into several rival cryptocurrencies (and related blockchains). Although they do occasionally occur, fork transactions are not currently the result of legal coercion.

Repeated forking involves the possibility of destroying Bitcoin’s standing as a stable(ish) currency.

It is well known that in Bitcoin and most other public blockchains there are no central, trusted authorities. Anyone in the world can perform transactions or become a miner.

For cryptographic computations to be performed, hardware and electricity must be available to all.

This openness is also a vulnerability, that opens the door to asymmetric threats and small-time malicious actors. Anyone can put information in the Bitcoin blockchain.

While nasty people will always exploit technology to do evil things, the blockchain fraternity should not succumb to a moral panic and fall in the trap of blaming the entire type of technology for that evil. This technique of including arbitrary data in the Bitcoin blockchain, that is irregularly being used perversely and unreasonably, is the same technique that is being used systematically every day to do socially beneficial record endorsing (notarizing), like tracking land title on the blockchain. The related elements of humanity should be careful not to criticize and disparage technology because of fear, uncertainty, and doubt.

Blockchains are a permanent record of any kind of data, from images to brief messages. Users should be aware that there is some danger involved because each participant must locally duplicate the entire blockchain, including any potentially hazardous material. Expert investigation finds that over 1600 files on the blockchain, more than 99-percent of which are texts or images, can make the mere ownership of a blockchain illegal due to certain content, such as illicit pornography. These files contain content that is clearly offensive and sly, including links to child pornography that is shared with all Bitcoin users.

# [Scammers](#toc.xhtml#s356a)

In general, **spam** is unsolicited email, instant messages, or social media messages. These messages are fairly easy to spot and can be damaging if you open or respond**. Scammers** use email or text messages to trick you into giving them your personal information. They may try to steal your passwords, account numbers, or Social Security numbers. If they get that information, they could gain access to your email, bank, or other accounts.

Scammers launch thousands of phishing attacks like these every day and often successful. **Phishing** is an email sent from an Internet criminal disguised as an email from a legitimate, trustworthy source. Phishing is a type of online scam that targets consumers by sending them an email that appears to be from a well-known source such as an internet service provider, a bank, or a mortgage company. It asks the consumer to provide personal identifying information.

Scams and phishing attacks come in many forms in the crypto world. Here is a list of some of the most common scams you should watch out for[^(\[5\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn5):

*   **Impersonation**: One might receive correspondence from someone posing as a Blockchain.com employee by phone, social media, or email. Workers never call or get in touch with on social media. All emails will originate from an official Blockchain.com email account that ends in blockchain.com or blockchain.info. Login credentials are not asked under any circumstances. Open a Support Center Ticket to verify the legitimacy of a request if you are unsure.
*   **Phishing Emails or Websites**: Always be wary when receiving an email that was not prompted by you. If an email asks you to go to a website and reset your password, do not do it unless you have specifically requested this. These types of scams often result in a malicious third party recording your login details and stealing your funds. Blockchain.com employees will never ask you for your Secret Private Key or Recovery Phrase. In case anyone asks you to provide this information, you can report it in their Support Center.
*   **Fake Investment Proposals**: This type of scam may ask you to “pay a fee” or “pay a tax” in order to release a bigger amount of funds to you. The scammers usually refer to official authorities such as the SEC or other institutions. Fraudsters can go as far as to forge official-looking documents or fake ID cards.
*   **Fake Refunds**: Blockchain.com does not provide refund services of lost funds from third-party platforms. If someone asks for payment in order to recover/refund lost or previously scammed funds from third-party platforms or old forgotten wallets, then this request is definitely a fraud. Payments are never asked for such services.
*   **Romance Scam**: This is a deceptive practice involving impersonation of an eventual partner or a promise of a fake relationship that can be used as a persuasion technique to manipulate victims in order to extract funds or sensitive data, such as login credentials, recovery phrases, and so on. Be very cautious when you come across a new person on social media.
*   **Money Transfers/Deposits/Withdrawals**: Never perform money transfers on behalf of another individual, either for them or by giving them your login details. Not only is this a potential violation of Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing regulations, but you are also putting your own funds at risk if you give someone access to your account.
*   **Platform Clones**: Be cautious of modified URLs such as b1ockchain.com, bl0ckchain.com, blockchain.io, and so on. Make sure you are using our official website with the correct URL to access your account. The login page of the Wallet platform is [https://login.blockchain.com/#/login](https://login.blockchain.com/#/login) and their Exchange platform is [https://exchange.blockchain.com/trade/login](https://exchange.blockchain.com/trade/login).

Other types of scams and phishing attacks could include blackmail, fake exchanges, fake airdrops, malware such as one that can affect Clipboard, an XLM transaction with malicious link as MEMO, Sim Swap, and “pump and dump” schemes, among others.

Please take care of the following as well:

*   Any email that ends in a domain other than blockchain.com or blockchain.info but appears to be from original is probably malicious.
*   It is probably a scam if you get a call from someone posing as an employee of Blockchain as they never make a call.
*   If an email ended up in your spam or junk mail folder, please check the email’s headers to be sure the sender is who they say they are.
*   Avoid clicking on links or downloading files from unsolicited emails or sources in general.

# [Transfer of Funds](#toc.xhtml#s357a)

The illicit practice of obtaining substantial sums of money through criminal conduct, such as sponsoring terrorism, honey trapping, or drug trafficking, is known as money laundering. Money laundering can be done in many different ways[^(\[6\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn6). If the quantity is within a realistic range of expectations, the easiest way to register these illicit gains is as sales by a genuine business. Some techniques for money laundering include:

*   Bulk cash smuggling
*   Black salaries
*   Gambling on cricket or any other game
*   Trade-based laundering
*   Round tripping

# [Preventing Money Laundering by Blockchain Technology](#toc.xhtml#s358a)

Blockchain technology possesses inherent characteristics that can potentially prevent money laundering. Every transaction done over blockchain leaves behind a permanent trail of records which is impossible to be altered. Thus, it makes easier for authorities to track the original source of the money[^(\[7\])](#c10.xhtml#ftn7).

In *[Chapter 1, Overview of Blockchain Technology](#c01.xhtml)*, we learned that a public blockchain ledger can supervise, validate, and record each transaction’s complete history. The in-built security of Blockchain system is such that the public ledger’s readers, members, and crypto miners get immediate notification of transactions as they occur. If all the transaction phases, including destination wallet, departure wallet, currency type, and amount, are anyhow left unverified, the transaction gets immediately blocked.

Blockchain can potentially be used to facilitate risk analysis and reporting for money laundering. Rather than only keeping an eye on entry and exit points, it enables comprehensive system analysis.

# [Immutable Ledger for Regulatory Oversight](#toc.xhtml#s359a)

The network is extremely secure since Blockchain is a decentralized system where every user or node must validate changes. Every node has access to the complete ledger record and is able to compare and identify any unlawful changes. Blockchain gives data integrity and immutability by removing unwanted modifications.

Because the ledger is verifiable and unchangeable, user trust is naturally incorporated. Blockchain easily and safely creates trust for any transaction facilitating the international transfer of funds or other sensitive data.

# [Transactions Monitoring in Smart Contracts](#toc.xhtml#s360a)

The integration facility of Smart Contracts in a Blockchain-based AML platform automates the process of fraud detection in the system. In-built algorithms keep a continuous check on every transaction, automatically generate an alert for a suspicious transaction, and immediately block the transaction. Thus, implementing blockchain in AML enables in gaining oversight over all the transactions.

# [Exchange of Blockchain](#toc.xhtml#s361a)

The company’s exchange is focused on mainstream adoption and ease of use.

Here, the bitcoin aspirant can view the markets, check the status of previously placed orders, can make deposits, withdraw funds, and trade crypto-assets. In some locations, you can also buy crypto using fiat. The Blockchain Exchange was built aiming towards the trader that is looking for a fast, intuitive, and simple trading experience.

A cryptocurrency exchange refers to a website or service where people may purchase, sell, or exchange fiat money for digital currency. This is in contrast to a cryptocurrency wallet, which is a program that allows an investor to keep all of their cryptocurrency.

Coinbase Global, Inc., selling the branded **Coinbase**, is an American company that operates a cryptocurrency exchange platform. Coinbase is a distributed company; despite not having a physical headquarters, every employee works remotely. By trading volume, it is the biggest cryptocurrency exchange in the US, handling over 30 different cryptocurrencies.

Coinbase Pro, the robust exchange powering Coinbase, is one of the largest and safest platforms out there.

According to the Bloomberg Billionaire Index, Changpeng Zhao—founder of cryptocurrency exchange Binance—is estimated to be worth $96 billion, making him the richest person in cryptocurrencies.

FTX is a fast-growing cryptocurrency exchange that offers advanced trading options such as margin and futures trading. While residents of the United States cannot use it due to strict regulations, a separate exchange—FTX.US—is available, but with fewer crypto offerings. FTX offers a wide array of services in addition to spot trading. It supports Bitcoin and Ethereum derivatives trading, as well as NFT trading. U.S. residents cannot trade on FTX’s global platform: due to strict regulations for the crypto space in the United States, residents of the U.S. have limited access to FTX. The exchange has a U.S. partner, FTX.US, but its offerings are more limited than the global platform.

Both FTX.US and Coinbase prioritize security and have excellent track records as of April 2022. Of these two, Coinbase has been around much longer without any data breaches or hacks. But each of these crypto exchanges does well from a security perspective. Two-factor authentication (2FA) is available on FTX.US and Coinbase.

# [MetaMask Exchange for Ethereum](#toc.xhtml#s362a)

The Metamask application includes an integrated service for exchanging **Ethereum** tokens by aggregating several decentralized exchanges (DEXs) to find the best exchange rate. This feature, branded as MetaMask Swaps, charges a service fee of 0.875% of the transaction amount.

MetaMask provides an essential utility for blockchain newcomers, token traders, crypto gamers, and developers. MetaMask provides the simplest yet most secure way to connect to blockchain-based applications. You are always in control when interacting on the new decentralized web-2. MetaMask generates passwords and keys on your device, so only you have access to your accounts and data. You always choose what to share and what to keep private. The attractiveness of Web 3.0 is that it is decentralized, meaning that rather than consumers accessing the internet through services mediated by companies such as Google, Apple or Facebook, individuals, themselves, own and govern sections of the internet. Web 3.0 ensures that its users always have control over their online data by utilizing decentralized networks. The next version of the internet is also expected to be more reliable due to its decentralized nature, which eliminates the possibility of a single point of failure. But it is very much different than VPN.

Web 2.0 (also known as participative or participatory web or social web) refers to websites that emphasize user-generated content, ease of use, participatory culture, and interoperability (that is, compatibility with other products, systems, and devices) for end users.

Web 3.0, also known as the next evolution of the World Wide Web. Some literature calls it as 4th gen Web. It provides a data-driven Semantic Web employing a machine-based understanding of data with the objective of developing a more intelligent and connected web experience for users. The three crucial foundations of Web 3.0 are Artificial intelligence (AI), Internet of things (IoT), and blockchain technology. Blockchain will help in achieving decentralized data storage and foster faith in the virtual world. Web3 is the name some technologists have given to the idea of a new kind of internet service that is built using decentralized blockchains — the shared ledger systems used by cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin and Ethereum. The term has been around for years, but it has come into Vogue in 2021 or so.

# [Frailty](#toc.xhtml#s363a)

Frailty is often defined as an aging-related syndrome of physiological decline, characterized by marked vulnerability to adverse health outcomes. Some bitcoin watchers, such as Swanson, also worry about the risk of one of the big exchanges being suddenly shut by authorities.

In July, U.S. authorities shut down the website of the BTC-e exchange, saying it had facilitated transactions involving ransomware, computer hacking, identity theft, tax refund fraud schemes, public corruption, and drug trafficking.

BTC-e, which is no longer operating, could not be reached for comment. The top three exchanges out of more than 100 - Bitfinex, GDAX, and bitFlyer - are home to more than 60 percent of all trading, according to data provider Bitcoinity.

The bitcoin blockchain will stop synchronizing in the event of an internet shutdown. The ledgers will immediately stop recording bitcoin transactions, leading to a temporary break in transaction processing. The network will resume if the transactions are consistent. A blockchain-based alternative can address this problem by replacing Internet giants with a decentralized, peer-to-peer network of providers. In such an alternative, members of the community would own and operate the infrastructure used for storage and computing.

What would happen if there was an internet shutdown? For the everyday person, some cell phone services and text messaging would be unavailable, all mobile apps and social networking sites would be down, cloud storage would be inaccessible, any pending electronic payments would fail, and more.

No person, company, organization or government runs the Internet. It is a globally distributed network comprising many voluntarily interconnected autonomous networks. It operates without a central governing body with each constituent network setting and enforcing its own policies. The cryptocurrency Bitcoin has raised financial concerns for governments worldwide. Despite its use for buying goods and services, there are still no uniform international laws that regulates Bitcoin.

Nowadays, virtual ledgers used by banks and other financial institutions hold 93% of the world’s money. There is no denying that we are moving towards a cashless future given the growing digitalization of financial services; the only question is, what that future will entail?

What is the lifespan of cryptocurrency?

Furthermore, blockchain projects only average a lifespan of roughly 1.22 years.

However, there is good news with the sharp rise in bitcoin use in 2021: illegal activity is at an all-time low. According to Chainalysis, the percentage of cryptocurrency transactions involving illegitimate addresses dropped to 0.15% in 2021 from 0.62% in 2020.

# [Metaverse](#toc.xhtml#s364a)

A metaverse is a network of 3D virtual worlds focused on social connection. In futurism and science fiction, it is often described as a hypothetical iteration of the Internet as a single, universal virtual world that is facilitated by the use of virtual and augmented reality headsets.

The term “metaverse” refers to a hybrid technology environment where users “live” in a virtual reality, augmented reality, and video. The metaverse’s proponents see their users engaging in anything from virtual international travel to concerts and conferences as well as working, playing, and maintaining social connections with friends.

The metaverse can be defined as a simulated digital environment that uses augmented reality (AR), virtual reality (VR), and blockchain, along with concepts from social media, to create spaces for rich user interaction mimicking the real world.

The metaverse has also been described as a place where users can connect, interact, and transfer themselves and their belongings across multiple digital locations. With the metaverse, customers will be able to take a 3D virtual hotel tour and research the location of the hotel where they are considering staying using VR platforms and hardware. Before booking a hotel room, travelers can put on their 3D digital avatars and take a virtual tour of the facility.

There is a debate about what the metaverse, the next-generation virtual reality-powered version of the Web, might look like. Yet despite the uncertainty of this hybrid physical/virtual landscape, the metaverse is inevitably going to be a fully functioning marketplace – among other things – where users can dart around from one place to another as digital replicas of themselves, purchasing products in virtual stores.

Although not owned by any one company – Google, Microsoft, and Samsung are also participating with Facebook with their involvement in the XR Association – Facebook has placed the biggest stake in this virtual land with an elaborate marketing campaign, which included renaming itself, Meta.

Think Ready Player One, but more accessible and pragmatic. Think of social media as it exists today, but more integrated and immersive. The notion of the metaverse – recently propelled into the mainstream by Facebook’s rebranding as Meta – will transform how we inhabit the world around us.

This has several implications:

*   In addition to two-dimensional digital spaces, users will now have an immersive reality that they can occasionally inhabit.
*   Content creators and designers (especially 3D modelling and VR world-building experts) can expect new opportunities on the road ahead.
*   The metaverse opens up a new economy where wealth can be created, traded, and enhanced using a currency distinct from but related to the real world.
*   The metaverse necessitates the evolution of new technologies for its execution. For example, you need a “Digital me” or the digital twin of a person to simulate real-world presence.
*   There are concerns around data privacy, security, diversity, and ethical behavior – problems of the real world that may take on a new dimension in a virtual one.

# [The Origins of the Metaverse](#toc.xhtml#s365a)

While the term metaverse may be new to some, it has been around as a concept for nearly three decades. American science fiction author Neal Stephenson introduced the metaverse in his 1992 novel, Snow Crash. In the novel (like others in its genre), users use metaverse as an escape from a futuristic, largely dystopian world.

Over the last 30 years, the industry has laid the foundations for a non-fictional metaverse, brick by brick. Virtual reality headsets have become more ergonomic and affordable. Global internet penetration has steadily risen. The popularity of online gaming, often reaching tournament levels, has made it possible to envision and execute sophisticated digital avatars, tradable digital assets, and even digital real estate.

Decentraland is a 3D virtual world browser-based platform. Users may buy virtual plots of land in the platform as NFTs via the MANA cryptocurrency, which uses the Ethereum blockchain. It was opened to the public in February 2020, and is overseen by the nonprofit Decentraland Foundation. The platform’s MANA coin is the cryptocurrency that is used to buy virtual goods and services, while LAND is an NFT token that defines ownership of land parcels that represent digital real estate. Decentraland is backed by $25.5 million in funding and provides users with a VR environment builder, customizable avatars, a crypto-enabled marketplace, digital wallets, and a governance system. Facebook is looking to build a similar experience, but one that intersects with pre-existing, non-AR/VR social media. It announced its pivot into AR/VR in September 2021, along with launching its own take on the metaverse.

# [Conclusion](#toc.xhtml#s366a)

All Fiat (the conventional) currencies, worldwide, derive their authority as being the media of transaction from the government or monetary authorities.

We have learnt about Legal status of cryptocurrencies in major countries of the world including India, USA, and China. El Salvador, EU, and Japan were the only countries in the world to allow Bitcoin as legal tender for monetary transactions. Japan’s Payment Services Act defines Bitcoin as legal property.

We have learnt about Transfer of Property and its related Acts, Non-Fungible Tokens (NFT) and its role in cryptocurrency.

In the next chapter, we will go through some practical questions related to blockchain network.

# [Containers](#toc.xhtml#s371a)

Programmable application environments that are totally separated are known as containers. In other words, they share the same operating system kernel but can each have its own networking interface, mounts, processes, and services, just like virtual machines.

At the heart of an operating system on a computer is a computer program called the kernel, which has total authority over all system functions. It is the area of the operating system code that is permanently stored in memory and allows the software and hardware to communicate with one another.

![](images/Figure-11.1.jpg)

**Figure 11.1:** Kernel of operating System

It is important to note that containers are not new with Docker.

Containers have been in existence for the past ten years. Among the various containers are LXC, LXD, LXCFS, and so forth. LXC containers are utilized by Docker. Because these container environments use very low-level code applications, the setup process is very complicated. On the other hand, Docker provides end users like us with a very easy-to-use collection of high-level tools that have multiple strong features.

![](images/Figure-11.2.jpg)

**Figure 11.2:** Containers and Docker Management

The docker technology utilizes several container technologies as shown in *[Figure 11.2](#c11.xhtml#fig11_2).*

# [Linux Containers (LXC)](#toc.xhtml#s372a)

Linux Containers (LXC) is designed to be an operating-system-level **virtualization** method for running multiple isolated Linux systems (containers) on a control host using a single Linux kernel. LXC is a well-known Linux container runtime that consists of tools, templates, and library and language bindings.

Linux containers are designed for running applications, whereas Virtual Machines are designed for running Operating Systems. Docker is developed in the Go language and utilizes LXC, cgroups, and the Linux kernel itself. Since it is based on LXC, a Docker container does not include a separate operating system; instead, it relies on the operating system’s own functionality as provided by the underlying infrastructure. Go was originally built for programs related to networking and infrastructure. It was intended to replace popular high-performance server-side languages such as Java and C++. Today, Go is used for a variety of applications such as cloud and server-side applications, DevOps, command line tools, and much more.

Control Groups (cgroups) is a Linux kernel feature that limits, accounts for, and isolates the resource usage (CPU, memory, disk I/O, network, and so on) of a collection of processes or process containers. One of the design goals of cgroups is to provide a unified interface to many different use cases, from controlling single processes (by using nice, for example) to full operating system-level virtualization.

# [The Docker Dashboard](#toc.xhtml#s373a)

The Docker Dashboard gives the user a quick view of the containers running on their machine. The Docker Dashboard is available for Mac and Windows. The user can simply manage the container lifecycle (stop, delete, and more), can easily access container logs, and get a shell inside the container.

The Docker Dashboard provides a simple interface that enables you to manage your containers, applications, and images directly from your machine without having to use the **Command Line Interface** (CLI) to perform core actions.

Remember that CLI, is a non-graphical, text-based interface to the computer system, where the user types in a command and the computer then successfully executes it. The **Terminal** is the platform or the IDE that provides the command line interface (CLI) environment to the user.

# [Docker Container](#toc.xhtml#s374a)

Docker is a set of platform as a service (PaaS) products that use OS-level virtualization to deliver software in packages called containers. The service has both free and premium tiers. The software that hosts the containers is called Docker Engine. It was first released in 2013 and is developed by Docker, Inc.

The following are the characteristic feature of the Docker Container**:**

*   An executable bundle of programs and their dependencies is called a Docker container.
*   All the instructions needed to run the solution are included in this package for the programmer.
*   The integrated structural redundancy makes it incredibly light.
*   The container is naturally transportable as well.
*   Its total isolation during operation is an additional advantage. Unlike with a virtual machine or non-containerized environment, it is guaranteed not to be affected by any host OS security measures or special configurations even if you are running a container.
*   The memory for a Docker environment can be shared across multiple containers, which is really useful, especially when you have a virtual machine that has a defined amount of memory for each environment.

The container is built using Docker images, and the command to run those images is Run.

Let us go through the basic steps of running a Docker image in this section for Practical 1 on Docker.

# [Docker’s Working Method](#toc.xhtml#s375a)

First, let us review some fundamental operating system ideas[^(\[1\])](#c11.xhtml#ftn1). Examine operating systems such as Windows, Fedora, Ubuntu, and CentOS, among others. They are all made up of an operating system kernel and a collection of programs. The software that runs on top of the operating system kernel (in this case, Linux) is what distinguishes different operating systems from one another. The operating system kernel is in charge of communicating with the underlying hardware.

Different user interface drivers, compilers, file managers, developer tools, and so on, may be included in the software. Thus, all operating systems use a similar Linux kernel, and some specialized software sets each operating system apart from the others.

Any OS version can be run on top of Docker, provided that each one is built upon the same kernel—in this case, Linux/Windows.

However, Docker is far more capable than this. Docker has the following features available:

*   Docker is an OS virtualized software platform that makes it easy for IT organizations to build, distribute, and run applications in containers that contain all of the dependencies. The container is actually a very compact package that includes all of the instructions and dependencies, such as libraries, bins, and frameworks.
*   It is relatively easy to shift the container itself from one environment to another.
*   When deploying a solution, you want to be able to ensure that the tested code will function as intended in the production environment. This is where Docker really shines in a DevOps life cycle. Furthermore, it is advantageous to have a container running the solution while you are developing and testing the code since it allows you to verify your work in the same environment that is used for production.

A server and a client are the two main components of the Docker engine, which is used to operate Docker. REST API is used for communication between the two components. The `instructComponents` of Docker are communicated by the server.

Docker can build images automatically by reading the instructions from a Dockerfile. A Dockerfile is a text document that contains all the commands a user could call on the command line to assemble an image. It executes any command on top of the current image and creates a new layer giving the result.

`RUN <command>` (the command is run in a shell)

`CMD`:- The `CMD` command defines a default command to run when your container starts.

Important instructions used in Dockerfile:

*   `RUN`: A RUN instruction is used to run specified commands. 
    
    `ENTRYPOINT`: The difference between `ENTRYPOINT` and `CMD` is that, if you try to specify default arguments in the docker run command, it will not ignore the `ENTRYPOINT` arguments. For example, `WORKDIR /usr/src/app`.
    
*   `EXPOSE`: 
    
    There are four components of Docker:
    
    *   Docker client
    *   Docker server
    *   Docker image
    *   Docker registry Docker Image

# [Docker Client and Server](#toc.xhtml#s376a)

This is a solution that requires you to utilize the terminal on your Mac or Linux computer to send commands to the Docker daemon from the Docker client. A REST API is used to facilitate communication between the Docker host and client. Similar instructions can be given, like the Docker Pull command, which instructs the daemon to carry out the task via interacting with other components (image, container, registry). In actuality, the Docker daemon functions as a server that communicates with the operating system and provides services. As one might expect, the Docker daemon continuously scans the REST API for requests to see if it needs to execute any particular ones. If you want to start the process from scratch, you can use the Docker command inside your Docker daemon to start all of your performances. The Docker daemon and registry ions to the client can then be executed on a Docker host.

# [Image Docker](#toc.xhtml#s377a)

A template with instructions for the Docker container is called a Docker image. Yet Another Markup Language (YAML), is the language used to write the template.

The YAML file contains the Docker image, which is then hosted as a file on the Docker registry. Docker-`compose.yml` is only utilized during the setup of local projects. There are multiple important layers in the image, and each layer is dependent on the layers below it. Each command in the Dockerfile is executed to produce an image layer, which is in read-only format. There is a layer of dependencies above your base layer, which is normally where your base image and base operating system are located. These then make up the read-only file that would eventually form your Dockerfile, including the instructions.

The instructions are delivered in four layers: `From`,` Pull`**,**` Run`, and` CMD`. Following is an explanation of their goal:

*   `From`: After a layer based on Ubuntu is created using the From command, files from the Docker repository are added to the base command of that base layer.
*   `Pull` : Adding files from your Docker repository is done using Pull.
*   `Run`: Constructs the container.
*   `CMD`: Indicates the command to execute inside the container. In this case, to run Python is the command. One of the things that will happen when we add more containers is that each new container will introduce a fresh layer of images to the Docker environment.

Since every container in the Docker environment is independent of every other container, you can write unique read-write instructions for every layer. It is interesting to note that removing a layer will also remove the layer above it.

# [The Docker Registry](#toc.xhtml#s378a)

The Docker registry is the location from which you would distribute and host different image formats. The repository is nothing more than an assembly of Docker images, which are created using YAML instructions and are incredibly simple to share and save. The Docker images can be named and tagged to make it simple for users to locate and distribute them inside the Docker registry. Using the publicly accessible Docker hub registry, which is open to everyone, is one method to begin administering a registry. For internal usage only, you can also establish your own register.

You can build both public and private photos for the registry that you internally establish. `Push` and `Pull` are the commands you would use to link the registry.

*   To push a newly generated container environment from your local management node to the Docker registry, use the push command.
*   To receive newly produced clients (Docker images) from the Docker registry, use the pull command.

Once more, a `Pull` command pulls and retrieves a Docker image from the Docker registry, while a `Push` command enables you to push a newly produced command to the registry—whether it be your own private registry or Docker hub.

# [Containerized Docker](#toc.xhtml#s379a)

Take a look at this simple Docker run command example, which launches a single redis container:

`$ Docker run redis`

It will be taken from the registry if the Redis image is not installed locally. Following this, you will be able to use the newly created Docker container Redis in your environment.

Let us now examine why containers are so thin. The reason for this is that they lack some of the extra layers that virtual machines have. The hypervisor is the most significant layer that Docker lacks, and it is not dependent on a host operating system.

The following is a list of prerequisites for creating and using Docker containers:

*   **Docker Engine**: This software builds and operates containers on the host computer.
*   **Docker Daemon**: This program controls Docker containers.
*   **Docker Client:** It executes instructions. The Docker Daemon receives the translated command over a REST API.
*   **Docker Compose**: This allows you to run two containers within one service.

# [Step-by-Step Docker Installation on Windows](#toc.xhtml#s380a)

Here are the step-by-step instructions for Docker installations on Windows:

1.  Go to the website [https://docs.docker.com/docker-for-windows/install/](https://docs.docker.com/docker-for-windows/install/) and download the Docker file. 
    
    ***Note:** A 64-bit processor and 4GB system RAM are the hardware prerequisites required to successfully run Docker on Windows 10.*
    
2.  Then, double-click on the `Docker Desktop Installer.exe` to run the installer. 
    
    ***Note**: Suppose the installer (`Docker Desktop Installer.exe`) is not downloaded; you can get it from Docker Hub and run it whenever required.*
    
3.  Once you start the installation process, always enable the Hyper-V Windows feature on the Configuration page. Docker installation on Windows Docker is an OS-level virtualization software platform that helps users build and manage applications in the Docker environment.
4.  Then, follow the installation process to allow the installer and wait until the process is done.
5.  After completion of the installation process, click Close and restart all its library dependencies.

# [Starting the Docker Desktop Tool](#toc.xhtml#s381a)

After the installation process is complete, the tool does not start automatically. To start the Docker tool, search for the tool, and select Docker Desktop in your desktop search results.

Before starting the application, Docker offers an onboarding tutorial. The tutorial explains how to build a Docker image and run a container. You are now successfully running Docker Desktop on Windows.

Next, follow the instruction below to install the Docker engine on your system. Go to Docker CLI and run the Docker version to verify the version of Docker installation on the system. You have already installed Docker Installation on Windows and now, you are ready to build and run Docker images and containers on the Docker ecosystem.

# [Getting Started with Docker Container](#toc.xhtml#s382a)

This section give a step-by-step instructions on how to get started with Docker.

# [Running Applications Using Docker Compose](#toc.xhtml#s383a)

If you are looking for information on how to containerize an application using your favorite language, see guides on Language-specific getting started.

**Downloading and Installing Docker**

This tutorial assumes you have a current version of Docker installed on your machine. If you do not have Docker installed, choose your preferred operating system below to download Docker:

*   Mac with Intel chip
*   Mac with Apple chip
*   Windows
*   Linux

If you have already run the command to get started with the tutorial, congratulations! If not, open a command prompt or bash window, and run the command:

`$ docker run -d -p 80:80 docker/getting-started`

You will notice a few flags being used. Here is an explanation of the aforementioned command:

*   `-d`**:** It runs the container in detached mode (in the background).
*   `-p 80:80`**:** It maps port 80 of the host to port 80 in the container. 
    
    docker/getting-started: The name of the Docker image from which the container will be created.
    

***Tip:** You can combine single character flags to shorten the full command. As an example, the command above could be written as:*

`$ docker run -dp 80:80 docker/getting-started`
