To access the dashboard, follow the instructions in the Docker Desktop manual.

# [Advantages of Working with Docker on Windows](#toc.xhtml#s385a)

Some of the top benefits of working with Docker on Windows are as below:

*   Enables integrated user interface to view and monitor Docker containers
*   Quickly starts Docker within ten seconds
*   Easy to use Linux Workspaces
*   Allots required resource and memory space
*   Consists of CA synchronization
*   Supports HTTP proxy settings

# [Container Revisited](#toc.xhtml#s386a)

Now that you have run a container, what is a container?

In short, a container is a process that is isolated from all other processes on the host machine and runs inside of a sandbox on your computer. This separation makes use of kernel namespaces and cgroups, two long-standing Linux features. Docker has made an effort to simplify and make these features accessible. In conclusion, a container:

*   Is an executable version of an image. The DockerAPI or CLI can be used to create, stop, relocate, or destroy containers.
*   May be installed in the cloud, run locally, or run in virtual machines.
*   Is OS-neutral (meaning it runs on any PC).

Run independently of one another, each container has its own software, binaries, and configurations.

**Container image:** It makes use of an isolated filesystem when operating a container. A container image provides this custom filesystem. All dependencies, configuration, scripts, binaries, and other components required to run an application must be included in the image since it houses the container’s filesystem. Additional container options, including environment variables, a default command to run, and other metadata, are also contained in the image.

# [Practical Exercise 2: Create and Deploy a Blockchain Network](#toc.xhtml#s387a)

*Create and deploy a blockchain network using the Hyperledger Fabric SDK for Java. Set up and initialize the channel, install and instantiate chaincode, and perform invoke and query on your blockchain network.*

# [Code Pattern](#toc.xhtml#s388a)

Blockchain is a secure, distributed, and open technology that is lowering costs, speeding up processes, and building a new level of trust in transactional applications. This developer code pattern gets you started with blockchain by showing you how to build a blockchain network using IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code and the IBM Blockchain Platform on IBM Cloud[^(\[2\])](#c11.xhtml#ftn2).

When something as big as a blockchain emerges, developers who understand and can implement the technology are in the driver’s seat. But first you have to learn it. In this developer pattern, the first in a series of “hello world” blockchain patterns, you will learn how to build a blockchain network using Hyperledger Fabric. You will create a smart contract that defines the participants, tradeable assets, and allowable transactions. You will then deploy the packaged contract onto a configured Hyperledger Network on the IBM Hyperledger Platform on IBM Cloud.

When you have finished this code pattern, you will understand how to:

*   Package up your blockchain smart contracts using the IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code.
*   Set up a Hyperledger Fabric network on IBM Blockchain Platform.
*   Install and instantiate a smart contract package through IBM Blockchain Platform.
*   Test out your blockchain network by executing a Node.js application with the Hyperledger Fabric SDK to interact with the deployed network by issuing transactions.
*   Discover how easy it is to set up and configure a basic network that includes a peer, orderer, channel, and certificate authority. Refer to the Getting Started with IBM Blockchain Platform in IBM Cloud Docs to learn more about these significant components of a blockchain. Business networks also include the participants who interact with them, each of which can be associated with a unique identity, across multiple business networks. This is your chance to climb on the blockchain train and build the skills every business will need. Complete this developer pattern, watch for future blockchain patterns, and make yourself indispensable.

![](images/Figure-11.3.jpg)

**Figure 11.3:** Architectural flow depiction of creating a node in Hyperledger Fabric Network

1.  The developer uses Node.js to create a smart contract.
2.  Use the IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code to package the smart contract.
3.  Configure and start the IBM Blockchain Platform application.
4.  The installation and instantiation of the smart contract on the network is made possible by the IBM Blockchain Platform, which facilitates the construction of a network onto an IBM Kubernetes service.
5.  The Node.js program communicates with the IBM Blockchain Platform’s deployed network and performs transactions via the Fabric Software Development Kit (SDK).

# [Networks of Blockchains](#toc.xhtml#s389a)

Applications can access ledger and smart contract services over a blockchain network, which is a type of technological infrastructure[^(\[3\])](#c11.xhtml#ftn3). The main function of smart contracts is to start transactions. These are then sent to all peer nodes in the network and permanently recorded on each one’s copy of the ledger. App users include end users who use client applications and blockchain network administrators.

A blockchain can track orders, accounts, payments, production, and a lot more.

Since all members have access to the same version of the truth, you can see every detail of a transaction from beginning to end, giving you more confidence as well as more opportunities and efficiency.

# [Blockchain Network Types](#toc.xhtml#s390a)

There are numerous approaches to building a blockchain network. They can be built by a consortium of persons or they can be private, public, authorized, or both.

# [Creating Blockchain Network-IBPV20 Using the IBM Blockchain Platform](#toc.xhtml#s391a)

A blockchain platform allows users and developers to create novel uses for an existing blockchain infrastructure. One example is Ethereum, which has a native cryptocurrency known as ether (ETH). But the Ethereum blockchain also allows the creation of smart contracts, programmable tokens used in initial coin offerings (ICOs), and non-fungible tokens (NFTs). These are all built up around the Ethereum infrastructure and secured by nodes on the Ethereum network.

# [Building a Blockchain Application Using the IBM Blockchain Platform](#toc.xhtml#s392a)

You will learn how to configure your Blockchain network on the IBM Cloud in Part 1. For novice developers, these will be the standard “Hello World” style Hyperledger samples utilizing the IBM Blockchain Platform. In this practical, the Hyperledger Fabric V1.4 network is utilized.

 The pattern of using the IBM Blockchain Platform extension for VS Code to package your smart contract and then publishing it into the network, demonstrates how to test your network.

One of the Hyperledger initiatives run by The Linux Foundation is the blockchain framework implementation known as Hyperledger Fabric. The Hyperledger Fabric is meant to serve as a basis for creating solutions or applications with a modular design that enables plug-and-play elements such as membership services and consensus. After completing this code pattern, you will know how to:

*   Use the IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code to package the smart contract.
*   Create a network using Hyperledger Fabric on the IBM Blockchain Platform.
*   Set up the IBM Blockchain Platform to host the smart contract bundle.
*   Use the SDK to interact with the contract and carry out transactions.

**IBM Blockchain Platform Components:**

*   With an intuitive user interface that can expedite and streamline the process of deploying and managing blockchain components on the IBM Cloud Kubernetes Service, the IBM Blockchain Platform grants you complete command over your blockchain network.
*   Highly available containers are deployed and a cluster of compute hosts is created by IBM Cloud Kubernetes Service. You can swiftly build, scale, and update apps by managing the resources you require with a Kubernetes cluster in a secure manner.
*   The IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code helps users create, test, and implement smart contracts, as well as establish connections with Hyperledger Fabric settings.

**Highlighted Functionalities:**

*   The distributed ledger platform of Hyperledger Fabric v1.4 is built on a modular design that offers excellent levels of secrecy, resilience, flexibility, and scalability.
*   The cross-platform, open-source Node.js JavaScript runtime environment runs JavaScript scripts on the server.

**Needs:**

*   A Cloud account with IBM.
*   Npm v6.0 or above with Node v8.x or v10.x.
*   Using VSCode version 1.38.0 or above.
*   VSCode API for the IBM Blockchain Platform Extension.

4.  Establish a network.

# [Practical Exercise 3: Interact with a Blockchain Network](#toc.xhtml#s395a)

*Execute transactions and requests against a blockchain network by creating an app to test the network and its rules ([https://developer.ibm.com/patterns/interacting-with-a-blockchain-network/](https://developer.ibm.com/patterns/interacting-with-a-blockchain-network/)).*

# [Developer Pattern](#toc.xhtml#s396a)

Blockchain is a secure, distributed, and open technology that is lowering costs, speeding up processes, and building a new level of trust in transactional applications. This developer code pattern gets you started with blockchain by showing you how to build a blockchain network using IBM Blockchain Platform Extension for VS Code and the IBM Blockchain Platform on IBM Cloud.

Any software design pattern is a general, reusable solution to a commonly occurring problem within a given context in software design. It is not a finished design that can be transformed directly into source or machine code.

A pattern (or design pattern) is a written document that describes a general solution to a design problem that recurs repeatedly in many projects. Software designers adapt the pattern solution to their specific project.

***Note:** This developer pattern is the first in a series of patterns designed to get you started with blockchain.*

Design patterns can speed up the development process by providing tested, proven development paradigms.

Every blockchain consists of a cluster of nodes functioning on a peer-to-peer (P2P) network system. Every node in a network has a copy of the shared ledger which gets updated timely. Each node can verify transactions, initiate or receive transactions, and create blocks.

Once you have your network, with participants and transactions defined, the next step is to create an application that tests that the network is set up correctly and operates as expected. One might call this a unit test, which repeatedly exercises the functionality of code. You should always build test cases to verify the logic of your code. In this case, you want to ensure that each participant can only execute transactions defined for that participant. Additionally, you want to validate that all the transactions are working as expected. This effort is fundamental to the development lifecycle process.

This developer code pattern helps you take what you built in the first step of this composite pattern, testing out the transactions. If you have not deployed the network in step 1, check out Explore Hyperledger Fabric through a complete set of APIs and set it up now. Then you are ready for this pattern, where you will build the application, deploy it, and step through issuing transactions.

![](images/Figure-11.13.jpg)

**Figure 11.13:** Flow Diagram for the Developer Code Pattern

# [Deploy Blockchain Network Smart Contract on the Network](#toc.xhtml#s397a)

1.  Install a smart contract.
2.  Navigate to the `Smart Contracts` tab in the left navigation and click `Install Smart Contract.`
3.  Browse to the location of the Blockchain Network smart contract package file (it is probably named blockchain-network@0.0.1.cds), which we packaged earlier using the IBM Blockchain Platform extension for Visual Studio code.
4.  Click on `Add file` and find your packaged smart contract.
5.  Once the contract is uploaded, click `Install Smart Contract`. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-11.14.jpg)
    
    **Figure 11.14:** Smart Contract Installation window
    

# [Instantiate Smart Contract](#toc.xhtml#s398a)

1.  Under `Installed Smart Contracts`, find the `Smart Contract` from the list (here, our is called blockchain-network) installed on our peer and click Instantiate from the overflow menu on the right side of the row.
2.  On the side panel that opens, select the channel, `mychannel,` on which to instantiate the smart contract. Click `Next`.
3.  Select the organization members to be included in the endorsement policy. In our case, we need to select `Org1MSP`. Click `Next`.
4.  We can skip the `Setup Private Data Collection` step and simply click `Next`.
5.  Give the `Function` name of instantiate and leave `Arguments` blank. 
    
    ***Note:** Instantiate is the method in the* `my-contract.js` *contract file that initiates the smart contracts on the peer. Some may name this* `initLedger`.
    
6.  Click `Instantiate`. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-11.15.jpg)
    
    **Figure 11.15:** Selecting a channel using Instantiate Contract window
    

# [Connect Application to the Network](#toc.xhtml#s399a)

1.  Connect with SDK through connection profile.
2.  Scroll down to the `Instantiated Smart Contracts` section and find the `"blockchain-network"` contract in the list. Click on `Connect with SDK` from the overflow menu on the right side of the row.
3.  From the dropdown for `MSP` for connection, choose `Org1MSP`.
4.  From the dropdown for `Certificate Authority`, choose `Org1 CA`.
5.  Download the connection profile by scrolling down and clicking `Download Connection Profile`. This will download the connection JSON which we will use to establish a connection between the Node.js web application and the Blockchain Network.
6.  You can click `Close` once the download completes. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-11.16.jpg)
    
    **Figure 11.16:** Connecting with SDK window
    

# [Create an Application Admin](#toc.xhtml#s400a)

1.  Navigate to the `Nodes` tab in the left navigation, and under `Certificate Authorities`, choose your organization `CA`, `Org1 CA`.
2.  Click on `Register user`.
3.  Give an `Enroll ID` of app-admin and `Enroll Secret` of `app-adminpw`. Set the `Type` for this identity as client. We can specify to use root affiliation or uncheck this field and select from any of the affiliated organizations from the drop-down list. We will leave the `Maximum Enrollments` field blank. Click `Next`.
4.  Under `Attributes`, click on `Add attribute`. Give attribute as `hf.Registrar.Roles`. This will allow this identity to act as a registrar and issue identities for our app. Click `Add attribute`.
5.  Click `Register user`. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-11.17.jpg)
    
    **Figure 11.17:** Register user window
    

# [Update Application Connection](#toc.xhtml#s401a)

1.  Copy the connection profile you downloaded into the application folder.
2.  Update the `config.json` file with:  
    *   The name of the downloaded connection JSON file.
    *   Your app admin’s enroll id and enroll secret, which we previously supplied as `app-admin` and `app-adminpw`.
    *   The `orgMSP` ID, `Org1MSP`, which we supplied.
    *   The `caName`, which is located under `"organization" -> "Org1MSP" -> certificateAuthorities"` in your connection’s JSON file. This would resemble a port and an IP address.
    *   The username you wish to set up.
3.  To connect to the IBM Blockchain Platform, update the gateway discovery to `{ enabled: true, asLocalhost: false }.`
    
    `{`
    
    `"connection_file": "mychannel_blockchain-network_profile.json",`
    
    `"channel_name": "mychannel",`
    
    `"smart_contract_name": "blockchain-network",`
    
    `"appAdmin": "app-admin",`
    
    `"appAdminSecret": "app-adminpw",`
    
    `"orgMSPID": "org1msp",`
    
    `"caName": "169.46.208.151:30404",`
    
    `"userName": "user1",`
    
    `"gatewayDiscovery": { "enabled": true, "asLocalhost": false }`
    
    ` }`
    

# [Run the Application](#toc.xhtml#s402a)

Enroll admin.

First, navigate to the application directory, and install the node dependencies.

`cd application`

`npm install`

Run the `enrollAdmin.js` script

`node enrollAdmin.js`

You should see the following in the terminal:

`msg: Successfully enrolled admin user app-admin and imported it into the wallet.`

`Run the invoke-transactions.js script to execute the transactions on the smart contract.`

`node invoke-transactions.js`

You should see the following in the terminal:

`Wallet path: /Users/snyk/CognitiveApps/code-patterns/Create-BlockchainNetwork-IBPV20/Create-BlockchainNetwork-IBPV20/application/wallet`

`Submit AddTrader transaction.`

`addTraderAResponse:`

`{"traderId":"traderA","firstName":"Carlos","lastName":"Roca"}`

`addTraderAResponse_JSON.parse:`

`{ traderId: 'traderA', firstName: 'Carlos', lastName: 'Roca' }`

`Submit AddTrader transaction.`

`addTraderBResponse:`

`{"traderId":"traderB","firstName":"Lisa","lastName":"Smith"}`

`addTraderBResponse_JSON.parse:`

`{ traderId: 'traderB', firstName: 'Lisa', lastName: 'Smith' }`

`Submit AddCommodity transaction.`

`addCommodityResponse:`

`{"tradingSymbol":"commodityA","description":"farm-commodity","traderId":"traderA"}`

`addCommodityResponse_JSON.parse:`

`{ tradingSymbol: 'commodityA',`

`description: 'farm-commodity',`

`traderId: 'traderA' }`

`Submit Commodity trade transaction.`

`commodityTradeResponse:`

`{"description":"farm-commodity","traderId":"traderB","tradingSymbol":"commodityA"}`

`commodityTradeResponse_JSON.parse:`

`{ description: 'farm-commodity',`

`traderId: 'traderB',`

`tradingSymbol: 'commodityA' }`

# [Troubleshooting](#toc.xhtml#s403a)

If you receive the following error on submitting transaction:

`error: [Client.js]: Channel not found for name mychannel`

It is safe to ignore this error because the IBM Blockchain Platform service has service discovery enabled by default. (In order to use service discovery to find other peers, please define anchor peers for your channel in the UI). If you really want the message to go away, you can add the channels section to the connection profile, but it is a warning rather than a true error telling the user the channel is found but not in the connection profile.

As an example, you can manually add the following JSON and update the IP address and ports manually:

`"channels": {`

`"mychannel": {`

`"orderers": [`

`"169.46.208.151:32078"`

`],`

`"peers": {`

`"169.46.208.151:31017": {}`

`}`

`}`

`},`

# [Extending the Code Pattern](#toc.xhtml#s404a)

This application can be expanded in a couple of ways:

Create a wallet for every member and use the member’s wallet to interact with the application.

Add a UI application in place of the invoke.js node application to execute the transactions.

# [Conclusion](#toc.xhtml#s405a)

In this chapter, we have completed three exercises to test our efficacy in learning Blockchain. We have first understood what is the need of Docker container and then installed it. The software that hosts the containers is called Docker Engine. We have understood in detail all the four components of Docker.

We have learnt the process of installing Docker on Windows.

In the second exercise, we learnt to create and deploy a Blockchain Network and the types of Blockchain Networks. For packaging the Smart Contract, we used the IBM Blockchain Platform extension on VS Code to package the smart contract.

We have understood how to interact with a Blockchain Network in the third exercise.
